<div class='question question'>

Work out $2898 \div 6$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 2898 \div 6 \\\\
&= \dfrac{2898}{6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{483 \times 6}{6} \\\\
&= \dfrac{483 \times \cancel 6}{\cancel 6} \\\\
&= 483
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

</div>

</li>

</div>
<div class='question question'>

Work out $\dfrac{5}{7}$ of $84$.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&=  \dfrac {5} {7} \ \text {of} \ 84 \\\\
&=  \dfrac {5} {7} \times 84 \\\\
&=  \dfrac {5 \times 84} {7} \\\\
&=  \dfrac {5 \times 12 \times 7} {7} \\\\
&=  \dfrac {5 \times 12 \times \cancel {7}} {\cancel {7}} \\\\
&=  5 \times 12 \\\\
&=  60
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$60$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$

&=  60
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{matrix}
&   \footnotesize {\maroonC {(+ 6)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(+ 12)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(+ 24)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(+ 48)} }
&&  \footnotesize {\maroonC {(+ 96)} }  \\
5,  &&   11,  &&  23,  &&   47, && \ldots, && \ldots
\end{matrix}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{First number}          &= 47 + 48 \\\\
                             &= 95 \\\\
\text{Second number}         &= 95 + 96 \\\\
                             &= 191
\end{aligned}
$

</div>

<div class='question question'>

The temperature inside Nanook's igloo is $9 \degree \text{C}$ and the temperature outside is $-12 \degree \text{C}$.

How many degrees warmer is it inside than outside? 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Difference}  &= 9 - (- 12)\\\\
                    &= 9 + 12 \\\\
                    &= 21 \degree \text {C}
\end{aligned}
$



</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$21 \degree \text {C}$

</div>
</div>

\begin{aligned}

                    &= 9 + 12 \\\\
                    &= 21 \degree \text {C}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Sherry's train to Bristol was scheduled to leave at $13{:}40$ and to arrive at $14{:}20$

However, the train left eight minutes late and then took $47 \ \text{minutes}$.

At what time did Sherry arrive?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Arrival time} &= 13{:}40 + 8 \ \text {minutes} + 47 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                    &= 13{:}40 + 55 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                    &= 14{:}35
\end{aligned}
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$14{:}35$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Arrival time} &= 13{:}40 + 8 \ \text {minutes} + 47 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                    &= 13{:}40 + 55 \ \text {minutes} \\\\
                    &= 14{:}35
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$14{:}35$
</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-2:1:14</p>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tnumber
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Lisa thinks of her favourite number.

She multiplies her favourite number by $2$, subtracts $3$ and gets $19$.


What is Lisa's favourite number?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let Lisa's favourite number $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \times a - 3      &= 19 \\\\
2a                  &= 19 + 3 \\\\
2a                  &= 22 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {22} {2} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {11 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {11 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                   &= 11
\end{aligned}
$

Lisa's favourite number $= 11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let Lisa's favourite number $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
2 \times a - 3      &= 19 \\\\
2a                  &= 19 + 3 \\\\
2a                  &= 22 \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {22} {2} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {11 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                   &= \dfrac {11 \times \cancel {2}} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a                   &= 11
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Lisa's favourite number $= 11$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$11$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Daniel and Bella are playing a game.

When Daniel calls out a number, Bella multiplies it by $3$ and then subtracts $5$ and writes down the result.

For example, when Daniel calls out $2$, Bella writes down $1$.

They record the numbers in a table.

Complete the table below.

| Daniel calls out | Bella writes down |
|:----------------:|:-----------------:|
|        $5$       |                   |
|                  |        $19$       |
|                  |        $-2$       |

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

| Daniel calls out                                                                                                 | Bella writes down                                                                                   |
|:----------------------------------------------------------------:                                                |:---------------------------------------------------:                                                |
|                                $5$                                                                               | $\green{5 \times 3 - 5 = 15 - 5 = 10} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(1)}}$ |
| $\green{\dfrac {19 + 5} {3} = \dfrac {24} {3} = 8} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(2)}}$ |                          $19$                                                                       |
| $\green{\dfrac {-2 + 5} {3} = \dfrac {3} {3} = 1} \ \maroonC{\footnotesize{(3)}}$  |                          $-2$                                                                       |

Completed table:

| Daniel calls out | Bella writes down |
|:----------------:|:-----------------:|
|        $5$       |        $10$       |
|        $8$       |        $19$       |
|        $1$       |        $-2$       |

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>| Daniel calls out                                                                                                 | Bella writes down                                                                                   |
|:----------------------------------------------------------------:                                                |:---------------------------------------------------:                                                |
|                                $5$                                                                               | $\rtq_katex_color_answer{5 \times 3 - 5 = 15 - 5 = 10} \ \rtq_katex_color_info{\footnotesize{(1)}}$ |
| $\rtq_katex_color_answer{\dfrac {19 + 5} {3} = \dfrac {24} {3} = 8} \ \rtq_katex_color_info{\footnotesize{(2)}}$ |                          $19$                                                                       |
| $\rtq_katex_color_answer{\dfrac {-2 + 5} {3} = \dfrac {3} {3} = 1} \ \rtq_katex_color_info{\footnotesize{(3)}}$  |                          $-2$                                                                       |
<emptyline>
Completed table:
<emptyline>
| Daniel calls out | Bella writes down |
|:----------------:|:-----------------:|
|        $5$       |        $10$       |
|        $8$       |        $19$       |
|        $1$       |        $-2$       |
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

Claire and Erin play a similar game.

They record their results in the table below.

| Claire calls out | Erin writes down |
|:----------------:|:----------------:|
|       $-1$       |        $1$       |
|        $0$       |        $4$       |
|        $1$       |        $7$       |
|        $2$       |       $10$       |

Work out what Erin does to each number that Claire calls out.

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Erin multiplies by $3$ then adds $4$

Let's verify.

| Claire calls out | Erin writes down                          |
|:----------------:|:-----------------------------------------:|
|       $-1$       |        $-1 \times 3 + 4 = -3 + 4 = 1$     |
|        $0$       |        $0 \times 3 + 4 = 0 + 4 = 4$       |
|        $1$       |        $1 \times 3 + 4 = 3 + 4 = 7$       |
|        $2$       |       $2 \times 3 + 4 = 6 + 4 = 10$       |

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

Erin multiplies by $3$ then adds $4$

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

The mass of the fibre in a pack of '*Salmon pasta*' is $7 \ \text{grams}$.

What is the mass of '*Salmon pasta*' in the whole pack?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the mass of salmon pasta $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \ \text{g} : 100 \ \text{g}                   &= 7 \ \text{g} : a \ \text{g} \\\\
\dfrac{2} {100}                                 &= \dfrac {7} {a} \\\\
2 \times a                                      &= 7 \times 100 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 100} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 50 \times 2} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 50 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                                               &= 7 \times 50 \\\\
a                                               &= 350 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Mass of salmon pasta $= 350 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{fibre}                            &:& \text{salmon pasta} \\\\
= & 2 \ \text{g}                            &:& 100 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {100} {2} \\\\
= & 7                                       &:& \dfrac {100 \times 7} {2} \\\\
= & 7                                       &:& \dfrac {2 \times 50 \times 7} {2} \\\\
= & 7                                       &:& \dfrac {\cancel 2 \times 50 \times 7} {\cancel 2} \\\\
= & 7                                       &:& 50 \times 7 \\\\
= & 7 \ \text{g}                            &:& 350 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Mass of salmon pasta $= 350 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$350 \ \text {g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 50 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
a                                               &= 7 \times 50 \\\\
a                                               &= 350 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Mass of salmon pasta $= 350 \ \text{g}$
</code></pre>

<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What will be the mass of fat in a pack of '*Salmon pasta*'?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the mass of fat $= a$

Using equivalent ratio,

$
\begin{aligned}
2 \ \text{g} : 5 \ \text{g}                     &= 7 \ \text{g} : a \ \text{g} \\\\
\dfrac{2} {5}                                   &= \dfrac {7} {a} \\\\
2 \times a                                      &= 7 \times 5 \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 5} {2} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {7 \times 5 \times 5} {2 \times 5} \\\\
a                                               &= \dfrac {175} {10} \\\\
a                                               &= 17.5 \ \text{g}
\end{aligned}
$

Mass of fat $= 17.5 \ \text{g}$

</div>
<div class='working'>

Using ratio,

$
\begin{array}{cccccccccccccc}
  & \text{fibre}                            &:& \text{fat} \\\\
= & 2 \ \text{g}                            &:& 5 \ \text{g} \\\\
= & 1                                       &:& \dfrac {5} {2} \\\\
= & 7                                       &:& \dfrac {5 \times 7} {2} \\\\
= & 7                                       &:& \dfrac {5 \times 7 \times 5} {2 \times 5} \\\\
= & 7                                       &:& \dfrac {175} {10} \\\\
= & 7 \ \text{g}                            &:& 17.5 \ \text{g}
\end{array}
$

Mass of fat $= 17.5 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$17.5 \ \text{g}$

</div>
</div>

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Children in class 6A who do not play tennis}     &= \text{Total children in class 6A} - \text{Children in class 6A who play tennis} \\\\
                                                       &= 23 - 13 \\\\
                                                       &= 10 \\\\
\text{Children in class 6B who play tennis}            &= \text{Total children in class 6B} - \text{Children in class 6B who do not play tennis} \\\\
                                                       &= 25 - 15 \\\\
                                                       &= 10 \\\\
\text{Number of children who play tennis}              &= \text{Total number of children} - \text{Number of children who do not play tennis} \\\\
                                                       &= 48 - 25 \\\\
                                                       &= 23
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Required fraction}      &= \dfrac {\text{Children in Class who do not play tennis}} {\text{Total children who do not play tennis}} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {15} {25} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {5 \times 3} {5 \times 5} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {\cancel 5 \times 3} {\cancel 5 \times 5} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {3} {5}
\end{aligned}
$

\end{aligned}
$

&= \dfrac {425 \times 134} {10 \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {56950} {10 \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5695 \cancel{0}} {1 \cancel{0} \times 2} \\\\
&= \dfrac {5695} {2} \\\\
&= 2847.5
\end{aligned}
$

<div class='question question'>

Barbara buys a box containing a selection of three types of biscuit.

There are eight chocolate biscuits.

A third of the other biscuits are custard creams.

There are twelve ginger biscuits. 

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

%empty%

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

</div>
</div>

</div>

</div>

</code></pre>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many custard creams are there?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>


Let the number of custard creams = a

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a                                &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times 12 \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac {12} {3} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac {4 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                                &= 4
\end{aligned}
$

Number of custard creams $= 4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>
Let the number of custard creams = a
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a                                &= \dfrac {1} {3} \times 12 \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac {12} {3} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac {4 \times 3} {3} \\\\
a                                &= \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
a                                &= 4
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Number of custard creams $= 4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$4$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many biscuits are in the box?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total number of biscuits  = t

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
t                                     &= 8 + 12 + \dfrac {1} {3} \times 12 \\\\
t                                     &= 20 + \dfrac {12} {3} \\\\
t                                     &= 20 + \dfrac {4 \times 3} {3} \\\\
t                                     &= 20 + \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
t                                     &= 20+ 4 \\\\
t                                     &= 24
\end{aligned}
$

Total number of biscuits $= 24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$24$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the total number of biscuits  = t
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
t                                     &= 8 + 12 + \dfrac {1} {3} \times 12 \\\\
t                                     &= 20 + \dfrac {12} {3} \\\\
t                                     &= 20 + \dfrac {4 \times 3} {3} \\\\
t                                     &= 20 + \dfrac {4 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} \\\\
t                                     &= 20+ 4 \\\\
t                                     &= 24
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Total number of biscuits $= 24$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>

<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-2:1:22</p>

<div class='topics'>

talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

In a box of shapes there are three times as many squares as there are circles.

There are twice as many triangles as squares.


If there are $45$ squares, how many shapes are there altogether?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the total shapes $=$ a 

Number of circles $= \dfrac {45} {3}$

Number of triangles $= 45 \times 2$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a                        &= 45 + \dfrac {45} {3} + 45 \times 2 \\\\
a                        &= 45 + \dfrac {15 \times 3} {3} +90 \\\\
a                        &= 45 + \dfrac {15 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} +90 \\\\
a                        &= 45 + 15 + 90 \\\\
a                        &= 150
\end{aligned}
$

Total shapes $= 150$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$150$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the total shapes $=$ a 
<emptyline>
Number of circles $= \dfrac {45} {3}$
<emptyline>
Number of triangles $= 45 \times 2$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
a                        &= 45 + \dfrac {45} {3} + 45 \times 2 \\\\
a                        &= 45 + \dfrac {15 \times 3} {3} +90 \\\\
a                        &= 45 + \dfrac {15 \times \cancel {3}} {\cancel {3}} +90 \\\\
a                        &= 45 + 15 + 90 \\\\
a                        &= 150
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Total shapes $= 150$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$150$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Which bus takes the shortest time from Elgin to Inverness and by how many minutes?

|           |          Bus P          |          Bus Q          |
|:---------:|:-----------------------:|:-----------------------:|
|   Elgin   | $11{:}47 \ \text{a.m.}$ | $12{:}33 \ \text{p.m.}$ |
| Inverness |  $1{:}06 \ \text{p.m.}$ |  $1{:}50 \ \text{p.m.}$ |


</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Difference}                   &= (1.06 - 11.47) - (1.50 - 12.33)  \\\\
                                    &= 1 \ \text{hour} \ 19 \ \text{minutes} - 1 \ \text{hour} \ 17 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                                    &= 2 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$



</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\text {Bus Q}$ $=$ $2 \ \text{minutes}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Difference}                   &= (1.06 - 11.47) - (1.50 - 12.33)  \\\\
                                    &= 1 \ \text{hour} \ 19 \ \text{minutes} - 1 \ \text{hour} \ 17 \ \text{minutes} \\\\
                                    &= 2 \ \text{minutes}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>

</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\text {Bus Q}$</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$2 \ \text{minutes}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Janet's marks on five mental arithmetic tests are:

$15 \quad 19 \quad 13 \quad 18 \quad 20$

What is her mean (average) mark?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Mean} = \dfrac {\text{Total of items}} {\text{Number of items}}$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average marks}            &= \dfrac {15 + 19 + 13 + 18 + 20} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {85} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {17 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                                &= 17
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$17$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_statistics_formula_mean
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Average marks}            &= \dfrac {15 + 19 + 13 + 18 + 20} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {85} {5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {17 \times 5} {5} \\\\
                                &= 17
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$17$

</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-2:1:27</p>

<div class='rag'>

<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
tprobability
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

Which is more likely, rolling a $3$ with an unbiased die with six faces, or getting a head with a fair coin?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Probability}  = \dfrac { \text{ Favourable outcomes} } { \text{Total outcomes} }$


$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(3)}     &= \dfrac {1} {6}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(head)}      &= \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1 \times 3} {2 \times 3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {3} {6}
\end{aligned}
$

Using the calculation above $\dfrac {3} {6} > \dfrac {1} {6}$.

So it is more likely to get a head with a fair coin than rolling a $3$ with an unbiased die with six faces.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\text {Getting a head with a fair coin}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_probability
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(3)}     &= \dfrac {1} {6}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{P(head)}      &= \dfrac {1} {2} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {1 \times 3} {2 \times 3} \\\\
                    &= \dfrac {3} {6}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Using the calculation above $\dfrac {3} {6} > \dfrac {1} {6}$.
<emptyline>
So it is more likely to get a head with a fair coin than rolling a $3$ with an unbiased die with six faces.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$\text {Getting a head with a fair coin}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How long, in metres, is a line of forty $10 \ \text{p}$ coins?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Length of forty 10p coins}   &= 40 \times 25 \ \text {mm} \\\\
                                   &= 40 \times \dfrac {25} {1000} \ \text {m} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {40 \times 25} {1000} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {40 \times 25} {40 \times 25} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {40} \times \cancel {25}} {\cancel {40} \times \cancel {25}} \\\\
                                   &= 1 \ \text {m}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$1 \ \text {m}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_md_unit_note
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Length of forty 10p coins}   &= 40 \times 25 \ \text {mm} \\\\
                                   &= 40 \times \dfrac {25} {1000} \ \text {m} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {40 \times 25} {1000} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {40 \times 25} {40 \times 25} \\\\
                                   &= \dfrac {\cancel {40} \times \cancel {25}} {\cancel {40} \times \cancel {25}} \\\\
                                   &= 1 \ \text {m}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$1 \ \text {m}$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the total value, in pounds, of forty $10 \ \text{p}$ coins?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin {aligned}
\text{Total value}       &= 40 \times 10 \ \text{p} \\\\
                         &= 40 \times \pounds \dfrac {10} {100} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {40 \times 10} {100} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {4 \cancel 0 \times 1 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
                         &= \pounds 4
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$\pounds 4$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>

                         &= \dfrac {40 \times 10} {100} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {4 \cancel 0 \times 1 \cancel 0} {1 \cancel 0 \cancel 0} \\\\
                         &= \pounds 4
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>

<div class='answercodeblock'>

</code></pre>

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

How many coins will Penny need for a $1 \ \text{kilometre}$ line of $10 \ \text{p}$ coins?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: It is really important to use the right units in calculations and convert where required.


$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Penny required}   &= \dfrac {1 \ \text {km}} {25 \ \text {mm}} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {1 \times 1000000 \ \text {mm}} {25 \ \text {mm}} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {1000000} {25} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {40000 \times 25} {25} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {40000 \times \cancel {25}} {\cancel {25}} \\\\
                         &= 40000
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$40000$

                         &= \dfrac {40000 \times 25} {25} \\\\
                         &= \dfrac {40000 \times \cancel {25}} {\cancel {25}} \\\\
                         &= 40000
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='workings'>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is Joanna's age, on $1 ^\text{st}$ January, in $2016$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Joanna's age}    &= 1 \text {st January} \ 2016 - 19 \text {th} \ \text {August} \ 2004 \\\\
					    &= 11 \ \text {years} \ 4 \ \text {months} \ 13 \ \text {days}
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$11 \ \text {years} \ 4 \ \text {months} \ 13 \ \text {days}$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Joanna's age}    &= 1 \text {st January} \ 2016 - 19 \text {th} \ \text {August} \ 2004 \\\\
					    &= 11 \ \text {years} \ 4 \ \text {months} \ 13 \ \text {days}
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>

<div class='answers'>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

In which year, on $1 ^\text{st}$ January, will Wendy's age be three times Joanna's age?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let Joanna's age in that year $= a$

Wendy's age in that year $= 3a$

As per the question,

$
\begin {aligned}
3a      &= a + 26 \\\\
3a - a  &= 26 \\\\
2a      &= 26 \\\\
a       &= \dfrac {26} {2} \\\\
a       &= \dfrac {2 \times 13} {2} \\\\
a       &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 13} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a       &= 13 \ \text {years}
\end {aligned}
$

Wendy will be $13 \ \text {years}$ in August of year $2004 + 13 = 2017$.

But we need to tell on $1 \text{st}$ January, so we need to increment the year by $1$.

$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Year}    &= 2004 + 13 + 1 \\\\
                &= 2018
\end {aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2018$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let Joanna's age in that year $= a$
<emptyline>
Wendy's age in that year $= 3a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
3a      &= a + 26 \\\\
3a - a  &= 26 \\\\
2a      &= 26 \\\\
a       &= \dfrac {26} {2} \\\\
a       &= \dfrac {2 \times 13} {2} \\\\
a       &= \dfrac {\cancel {2} \times 13} {\cancel {2}} \\\\
a       &= 13 \ \text {years}
\end {aligned}
$
<emptyline>
Wendy will be $13 \ \text {years}$ in August of year $2004 + 13 = 2017$.
<emptyline>
But we need to tell on $1 \text{st}$ January, so we need to increment the year by $1$.
<emptyline>
$
\begin {aligned}
\text {Year}    &= 2004 + 13 + 1 \\\\
                &= 2018
\end {aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question subquestion'>

What percentage of the girls play in the hockey team but not in the netball team?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Percentage of girls}                  &=  \dfrac {7} {25} \times 100 \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {7 \times 100} {25} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {7 \times 4 \times 25} {25} \\\\
                                            &= \dfrac {7 \times 4 \times \cancel{25}} {\cancel{25}} \\\\
                                            &= 7 \times 4 \\\\
                                            &= 28\%
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$28\%$

</div>

                                            &= \dfrac {7 \times 4 \times \cancel{25}} {\cancel{25}} \\\\
                                            &= 7 \times 4 \\\\
                                            &= 28\%
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

The diagram shows two squares. The larger square has perimeter $16 \ \text{cm}$.

![the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-2/section-1-question-35-00.png](/assets/the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-2/section-1-question-35-00.png "the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-2/section-1-question-35-00.png")


What is the **area** of the smaller, white square?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of square} = \text{side} \times \text{side}$


ABBR: $\text{Perimeter of square} = 4 \times \text{side}$


$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times a &= 16 \ \text{cm} \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {16} {4} \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {4 \times 4} {4} \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a          &= 4 \ \text{cm} 
\end{aligned}          
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Area of bigger square}  &= 4 \times 4 \\\\
                              &= 16 \ \text{cm}^2 \\\\
\text{Area of smaller square} &= \dfrac{\text{Area of bigger square}}{2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{16} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {8 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                              &= 8 \ \text{cm}^2
\end{aligned}          
$


</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$8 \ \text{cm}^2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_square_expanded
<emptyline>
rtq_abbr_katex_formula_expanded_perimeter_square
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4 \times a &= 16 \ \text{cm} \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {16} {4} \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {4 \times 4} {4} \\\\
a          &= \dfrac {\cancel 4 \times 4} {\cancel 4} \\\\
a          &= 4 \ \text{cm} 
\end{aligned}          
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Area of bigger square}  &= 4 \times 4 \\\\
                              &= 16 \ \text{cm}^2 \\\\
\text{Area of smaller square} &= \dfrac{\text{Area of bigger square}}{2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac{16} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {8 \times 2} {2} \\\\
                              &= \dfrac {8 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\
                              &= 8 \ \text{cm}^2
\end{aligned}          
$
<emptyline>
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>

</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$8 \ \text{cm}^2$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the **maximum** number of matchboxes that could fit, in one layer, onto a tray that is 
$20 \ \text{cm}$ long and $15 \ \text{cm}$ wide?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Area of rectangle} = \text{length} \times \text{width}$


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of Matchboxes}    &= \dfrac {\text {Area of tray}} {\text {Area of the base of matchbox}} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {20 \times 15} {3 \times 5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {20 \times 3 \times 5} {3 \times 5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel {3} \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                                &= 20
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_area_rectangle_expanded
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of Matchboxes}    &= \dfrac {\text {Area of tray}} {\text {Area of the base of matchbox}} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {20 \times 15} {3 \times 5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {20 \times 3 \times 5} {3 \times 5} \\\\
                                &= \dfrac {20 \times \cancel {3} \times \cancel {5}} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                                &= 20
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$20$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

What is the maximum number of matchboxes that could be fitted into a box measuring $18 \ \text{cm}$ by $25 \ \text{cm}$ by $10 \ \text{cm}$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

ABBR: $\text{Volume of a cuboid} = \text{length} \times \text{width} \times \text{height}$


As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of matchboxes}            &= \dfrac {\text {Volume of box}} {\text {Volume of a matchbox}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {18 \times 25 \times 10} {1 \times 3 \times 5} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 6 \times 5 \times 5 \times 10} {3 \times 5} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 6 \times \cancel {5} \times 5 \times 10} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                                        &= 6 \times 5 \times 10 \\\\
                                        &= 300
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$300$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>rtq_abbr_katex_formula_volume_cuboid
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of matchboxes}            &= \dfrac {\text {Volume of box}} {\text {Volume of a matchbox}} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {18 \times 25 \times 10} {1 \times 3 \times 5} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {3 \times 6 \times 5 \times 5 \times 10} {3 \times 5} \\\\
                                        &= \dfrac {\cancel {3} \times 6 \times \cancel {5} \times 5 \times 10} {\cancel {3} \times \cancel {5}} \\\\
                                        &= 6 \times 5 \times 10 \\\\
                                        &= 300
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$300$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question question'>

In order to convert from the imperial units ounces and pounds to the metric unit kilograms, you should use the following conversions:

$
\begin{aligned}
16 \ \text{ounces} &= 1 \ \text{pound}\\
2.2 \ \text{pounds} &= 1 \ \text{kg}
\end{aligned}
$

Newborn tiger cubs weigh about $56 \ \text{ounces}$.

Circle the mass in kilograms which gives the best approximation of the mass of a newborn tiger cub.

$0.5 \ \text{kg} \quad 1 \ \text{kg} \quad 1.5 \ \text{kg} \quad 2 \ \text{kg} \quad 2.5 \ \text{kg} \quad 3 \ \text{kg}$

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
&= 56 \ \text{ounces} \\\\
&= (56 \div 16) \ \text{pounds} \\\\
&= 3.5 \ \text{pounds} \\\\
&= (3.5 \div 2.2) \ \text{kg} \\\\
&= 1.59 \ \text{kg} \\\\
&= 1.5 \ \text{kg} \ \text{(approx.)}
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
<div class='working placeholder'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

1.5 kg

</div>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

TODOANSWER

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
&= 56 \ \text{ounces} \\\\
&= (56 \div 16) \ \text{pounds} \\\\
&= 3.5 \ \text{pounds} \\\\
&= (3.5 \div 2.2) \ \text{kg} \\\\
&= 1.59 \ \text{kg} \\\\
&= 1.5 \ \text{kg} \ \text{(approx.)}
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>TODOWORKING
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

<div class='question question'>

Two icebergs, $A$ and $B$, are floating in the ocean.

On $1$ January, iceberg $A$ weighs $4 \ \text{tonnes}$, but loses $25 \ \text{kg}$ every day.

$1000 \ \text{kg} = 1 \ \text{tonne}$

</div>




<ul class='subquestion lower-alpha question_type_subquestion list_type_lower-alpha'>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

After how many days will iceberg $A$ weigh $3850 \ \text{kg}$?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Lost weight}  &= 4000 - 3850 \\\\
                    &= 150 \text{ kg}
\end{aligned}
$

$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of days}  &= \dfrac{150}{25} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac{25 \times 6}{25} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac{\cancel{25} \times 6}{\cancel{25}} \\\\
                       &= 6
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$6$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Lost weight}  &= 4000 - 3850 \\\\
                    &= 150 \text{ kg}
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
\text{Number of days}  &= \dfrac{150}{25} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac{25 \times 6}{25} \\\\
                       &= \dfrac{\cancel{25} \times 6}{\cancel{25}} \\\\
                       &= 6
\end{aligned}
$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$6$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_not_found rag_not_found subquestion'>
<div class='question subquestion'>

On $1$ January, iceberg $B$ weighs $4500 \ \text{kg}$ and loses $50 \ \text{kg}$ every day.

After how many days will the two icebergs have the same mass?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of days $= a$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
4000 - 25 \times a  &=  4500 - 50 \times a  \\\\
4000 - 25a          &=  4500 - 50a  \\\\
50a - 25a           &=  4500 - 4000  \\\\
25a                 &=  500  \\\\
a                   &=  \dfrac{500}{25}  \\\\
a                   &=  \dfrac{20 \times \cancel{25}}{\cancel{25}}  \\\\
a                   &=  20
\end{aligned}
$

After $20$ days, the two icebergs will have the same mass.

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$20$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of days $= a$
<emptyline>
As per the question,
<emptyline>
$
\begin{aligned}
4000 - 25 \times a  &=  4500 - 50 \times a  \\\\
4000 - 25a          &=  4500 - 50a  \\\\
50a - 25a           &=  4500 - 4000  \\\\
25a                 &=  500  \\\\
a                   &=  \dfrac{500}{25}  \\\\
a                   &=  \dfrac{20 \times \cancel{25}}{\cancel{25}}  \\\\
a                   &=  20
\end{aligned}
$
<emptyline>
After $20$ days, the two icebergs will have the same mass.
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
</div>
<div class='answercodeblock'>
<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>$20$
</code></pre>
</div>
</div>

</div>
</li>
</ul>

<div class='question question'>

A number machine works to the rule

'*cube each digit and then add the cubes together*'.

For example:

input $46$ gives output $280$, as shown below

$
\begin{aligned}
   &= 4^\text{3} + 6^\text{3} \\
   &= 64 + 216 \\
   &= 280
\end{aligned}
$

input $123$ gives output $36$

$
\begin{aligned}
   &= 1^\text{3} + 2^\text{3} + 3^\text{3} \\
   &= 1 + 8 + 27 \\
   &= 36
\end{aligned}
$


</div>

</div>
<div class='working placeholder'>

TODOWORKING

</div>
</div>

</div>
<div class='answer placeholder'>

</div>

<li class='questionContainer'>
<div class='question_envelope rag_g1 rag_prns question'>
<div class='uuid'>
<p>C918F898-DCE1-4B0B-95FA-F2465D672E52</p>
</div>
<div class='papername'>
<p>the-london-independent-girls-schools-consortium--11-plus--maths--2016--group-2:1:45</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>Waffles Woof</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_g1</p>
</div>
<div class='rag'>
<p>rag_wf_prns</p>
</div>
<div class='topics'>
<ul>
<li>
talgebra
</li>
</ul>
</div>
<div class='question question'>

A bird has $2$ legs, a cat has $4$ legs, an insect has $6$ legs and a spider has $8$ legs.

Claire looks at some animals and counts all their legs.

She counts $38$ legs.

There are twice as many birds as spiders and twice as many cats as insects.

How many of each type of animal can she see?

</div>
<div class='workings'>
<div class='working'>

Let the number of spiders $=a$

Number of birds $=2a$

Let the number of insects $=b$

Number of cats $=2b$

As per the question,

$
\begin{aligned}
a \times 8 + 2a \times 2 + b \times 6 + 2b \times 4 &= 38 \\\\
8a + 4a + 6b + 8b                                   &= 38 \\\\
12a + 14b                                           &= 38 \\\\
2 \times (6a + 7b)                                  &= 38 \\\\
(6a + 7b)                                           &= \dfrac{38} {2} \\\\ 
6a + 7b                                             &= \dfrac{19 \times 2} {2} \\\\  
6a + 7b                                             &= \dfrac{19 \times \cancel 2} {\cancel 2} \\\\ 
6a + 7b                                             &= 19     
\end{aligned}
$

We can easily infer the values by using trial and error. Let's find out $2$ numbers which satisfies the equation above.


| Value of $a$  |  Value of $b$   |    $6a + 7b$         |
|:---------:    |:--------------: |:------------------:  |
| $1$           |      $1$        |       $13$           |
| $2$           |      $1$        |       $19$           |

A quick check shows $2$ and $1$ satisfies the above equation.

$
\begin{aligned}
\text {Number of spiders} &= 2 \\\\
\text {Number of birds}   &= 2 \times 2 \\\\
                          &= 4 \\\\
\text {Number of insects} &= 1 \\\\
\text {Number of cats}    &= 1 \times 2 \\\\
                          &= 2
\end{aligned}
$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>
<div class='answer'>

$2 ,4 , 1, 2$

</div>
</div>
<div class='workingscodeblock'>
<div class='workingcodeblock'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>Let the number of spiders $=a$
<emptyline>
Number of birds $=2a$
<emptyline>

<emptyline>

$

</div>
</div>
<div class='answers'>

<pre class='language-latex'><code class='language-latex'>%empty%</code></pre>
